But it contradicts itself on dates, says 'free to the person eating' three times without one number, and hands a short-route reader terms (door, Monday, ASI, USI, land trusts, 'Wide lens, narrow focus') that only the skipped chapters explain.

**Publishable as is?** Only with fixes. At block level, drop b0050/b0051 (a ledger figure with no numbers), b0018 (abstract table), b0038 (name trivia), b0044 (committee prose) and b0024 (an orphaned motto), and keep the definition, plan, stages and objections. The date contradiction (b0041 against b0021) is a book-level stance CT has to resolve in v0.12. The cut can't fix it except by dropping b0041.

### Failure modes

- **critical**: The condensed book has no hook. A newcomer on the short route gets a summary written for someone who already read the book. No Elijah, no 'we don't have to live like this', no reason to care before the stage list. (sts.appendix-c.b0036, sts.appendix-c.b0027) Quote (CT author voice): "Use AI and robots to make survival more secure."
- **major**: It contradicts itself on dates. b0021 declines Accelerating Change because 'A curve gives you a date', and Appendix D's P-03 says 'Plan by doors, not dates'. Then b0041 gives a date to the day. A hostile host will play it on November 26, 2027. (sts.appendix-c.b0021, sts.appendix-c.b0041) Quote (CT author voice (third person)): "The author's personal AGI forecast is U.S. Thanksgiving, November 25, 2027."
- **major**: 'Free to the person eating' appears three times (b0029, b0051, b0049), with no cost, no number and no mechanism. The figure promises a ledger, and its own alt text admits it shows no prices. The 'Who pays for it?' answer never says 'tax'. Its precedent is a repaid capital loan that members paid off through their electric bills, which is the opposite of free at the point of use. (sts.appendix-c.b0029, sts.appendix-c.b0050, sts.appendix-c.b0051, sts.appendix-c.b0049) Quote (CT author voice (figure alt text)): "No prices or budget estimates are shown."
- **major**: Two nine-item sequences in a row: the nine stages, then nine plan lines that Chapter 19 calls rungs. They invite conflation, and Appendix H later asks about both 'the nine stages' and 'the nine rungs'. (sts.appendix-c.b0042, sts.appendix-c.b0043, sts.appendix-c.b0046, sts.appendix-c.b0047) Quote (CT author voice): "Chapter 19 lays it out as a ladder."
- **major**: Undefined terms for the very reader this page serves. b0041 says 'Chapter 1 says what would count against it', and the short route skips Chapter 1. 'Wide lens, narrow focus' is set up in Chapter 18, which the short route also skips. (sts.appendix-c.b0021, sts.appendix-c.b0040, sts.appendix-c.b0043, sts.appendix-c.b0047, sts.appendix-c.b0024, sts.appendix-c.b0041) Quote (CT author voice): "A curve gives you a date. A door gives you a Monday."
- **major**: The stage name reads as contempt out of context. Chapter 2 says the primates are all of us. Appendix C keeps the label and drops that disclaimer, so a worker on the short route sees his anger filed under primates. (sts.appendix-c.b0043) Quote (CT author voice): "Stage 5: The Primate Backlash."
- **minor**: Speculative Stages 6 to 9 sit in an executive brief. Superintelligence leaving and intelligence at cosmic scale undercut credibility with the policy reader this brief is meant to be handed to. (sts.appendix-c.b0043) Quote (CT author voice): "Speculation: intelligence at the scale of the cosmos."
- **minor**: Third-person 'the author' breaks the first-person contract the rest of the book keeps. Appendix G even says 'Add mine.' (sts.appendix-c.b0041, sts.appendix-c.b0042) Quote (CT author voice (third person)): "Stages 1 through 5 organize the near-term choices the author wants us to make; they aren't an established sequence."
- **minor**: The abstract four-question table and the five-name list add length without helping anyone explain the book in ten minutes. (sts.appendix-c.b0018, sts.appendix-c.b0038, sts.appendix-c.b0028) Quote (CT author voice): "Useful work under real physical and organizational conditions."

### What readers would complain about

- *bored newcomer on the short route*: "The 'short route' sent me here to get the book in ten minutes. I got a definition, three schools, a forecast, nine stages, nine more lines and seven objections. Where's the part that makes me care?"
- *hostile podcast host*: "First he says a curve gives you a date and dates are useless. Four headings later: 'U.S. Thanksgiving, November 25, 2027.' Pick one."
- *fiscal skeptic*: "'Who pays for it?' 'Everyone together.' That's a tax with the word tax taken out. And the co-ops charged for electricity."
- *singularity regular*: "It name-checks Yudkowsky's three schools and never mentions the thing Yudkowsky is famous for. A superintelligence 'might simply leave.' Sure."
- *union member who just lost hours*: "I'm angry about my job and the book files me under 'The Primate Backlash.'"
- *city council staffer handed the brief*: "Stage 4 is food. Stage 9 is 'The Apex Intelligence' and 'the simulation question.' I can't put this in front of my council member."

### Weak writing

- `sts.appendix-c.b0039` "It doesn't wait for a superintelligence, you can test it on your own paycheck this quarter, and it points at the real emergency: the gap between the contract that's failing and the one nobody has drafted." : A comma splice whose subjects shift mid-list ('It', 'you', 'it'). It's a list of three for rhythm, and it overclaims: the brief never says how you test it on a paycheck.
- `sts.appendix-c.b0040` "Intelligence makes technology; aim it at itself and the loop closes. A claim about feedback." : It breaks the parallel the first two bullets set up ('It ends in a date.' / 'It ends in a door.'). The reader waits for a third ending that never comes.
- `sts.appendix-c.b0021` "A curve gives you a date. A door gives you a Monday." : An aphorism that only works if you've read Chapter 1. To a short-route newcomer it's a riddle.
- `sts.appendix-c.b0018` "Tested improvements to its own successors, including the failures." : Academic drift and stacked abstractions. It assumes the reader knows recursive self-improvement.
- `sts.appendix-c.b0044` "A small trial can test a task; a continuing service needs money, responsibility, and terms people can rely on." : A list of three and committee voice. 'Responsibility' and 'terms' are abstractions doing no work.
- `sts.appendix-c.b0042` "they aren't an established sequence." : A hedge that undercuts the map in the same breath that introduces it.
- `sts.appendix-c.b0049` "Help should widen a life, not shrink it, and that's something we can watch and change (Chapter 11)." : A 'not X' slogan answering a real fear. 'Something we can watch and change' is vague.
- `sts.appendix-c.b0051` "Free to the person eating still leaves a working ledger: equipment, maintenance, remaining labor, and delivery need dependable funding." : The caption repeats the paragraph two blocks above it almost word for word, and the same phrase comes back a third time in b0049.
- `sts.appendix-c.b0024` "Wide lens, narrow focus." : An orphaned motto. It explains nothing here and ends the brief on a fortune cookie.

### Not in your voice

- `sts.appendix-c.b0041` "The author's personal AGI forecast is U.S. Thanksgiving, November 25, 2027." : CT's draft speaks as I ('I vote for the latter', 'These are the questions that keep me up at night'). Calling himself 'the author' reads like a press kit.
- `sts.appendix-c.b0044` "Bring workers, growers, cooks, recipients, and public decision-makers into the answer." : Committee diction. The draft addresses one scared person directly: 'You're afraid. I get it. Who wouldn't be?'
- `sts.appendix-c.b0018` "Evidence that a system does unfamiliar tasks reliably, checked independently." : Lab-report register. The draft explains AI with a picture: 'Imagine a world where Einstein is your entry-level tech support.'
- `sts.appendix-c.b0027` "Use AI and robots to make survival more secure." : A flat policy opener. The draft opens with a joke and a jolt, and this is the page newcomers meet first.

### Where a newcomer gets lost

- `sts.appendix-c.b0021`: 'Door' and 'Monday' are never defined here. b0040 says Event Horizon 'ends in a door' without saying what a door is.
- `sts.appendix-c.b0040`: 'Smart' (John Smart) is a bare surname. Toffler and Kurzweil are assumed knowledge.
- `sts.appendix-c.b0043`: ASI and USI are never expanded in this appendix.
- `sts.appendix-c.b0031`: 'Land care' and 'cleanup' are unexplained (cleanup of what?), and nothing argues why shelter comes fourth, behind them.
- `sts.appendix-c.b0047`: 'Land trusts' and 'the way farmers owned their power lines' both need Chapter 19.
- `sts.appendix-c.b0024`: The motto's setup is in Chapter 18, which the short route skips.
- `sts.appendix-c.b0041`: It sends the reader to Chapter 1 for the falsifier, but the short route skips Chapter 1, so this reader gets the date without the conditions.

### Skim problems

- Two nine-item lists in a row with different meanings (stages b0043, plan lines b0047).
- The four-question table (b0018) has no lead-in, so a skimmer can't tell what the questions are about.
- The only figure (b0050) is a ledger with no numbers in it, an image that says nothing at a glance.
- The best skim unit, the objections list (b0049), sits near the end, after 900 words of definitions and stages.

### Best blocks

- `sts.appendix-c.b0039`: The book's best idea in two bullets. 'Intelligence stops being scarce, and every institution quietly built on its scarcity starts failing' is testable and original, and it gives a newcomer the frame.
- `sts.appendix-c.b0019`: 'Two people defending different claims with the same noun' clears the definitional fog in one sentence.
- `sts.appendix-c.b0049`: The most useful page for anyone defending the book at dinner. 'What they don't want is to spend forty years renting the chance to survive' is CT at full strength.
- `sts.appendix-c.b0047`: The plan as nine verbs. Concrete, skimmable and quotable.
- `sts.appendix-c.b0043`: Despite its problems, it carries real CT lines: 'Grief with a brick in its hand' and 'None of it changes what's for dinner.'
- `sts.appendix-c.b0029`: 'Cheaper production doesn't settle who gets access; people do' is the book's thesis in one line.


## Appendix D: The Precedent Ledger

**Verdict.** An operating manual for 24 precedents that live elsewhere. If the core cut drops the chapter-closing precedents, this appendix indexes nothing and its rules become fortune cookies. The symptom index and the blacklist are the two best pages in the back matter. Both are buried under a seven-step self-help ritual, a title table, a timeline that duplicates the table, and a caption that sends you to Appendix D while you are in Appendix D.

**Publishable as is?** Only with fixes. The figure (b0028 alt text) and caption (b0029) point the reader to the appendix they're already in, so drop both blocks. The appendix is publishable at all only if the precedents survive in the chapters. If they're cut, keep b0027 at most, because sts.chapter7.b0104 sends readers to it.

### Failure modes

- **critical**: Total dependency on precedent blocks elsewhere in the book. Every table row, rule and symptom points to a chapter closer. In a 50,000-word cut that trims precedents, the ledger points at nothing. (sts.appendix-d.b0015, sts.appendix-d.b0018, sts.appendix-d.b0022, sts.appendix-d.b0023, sts.appendix-d.b0024) Quote (CT author voice): "The serial numbers run in reading order, P-01 through P-24."
- **major**: A self-referential proofing bug. The timeline figure sits inside Appendix D and tells the reader that the titles and dates are in Appendix D. A reviewer will cite it as proof nobody read the back matter. (sts.appendix-d.b0028, sts.appendix-d.b0029) Quote (CT author voice (caption)): "Square-ended dashed bars mark approximate periods; exact dates and complete titles are in Appendix D."
- **major**: The page says it's meant to be opened mid-situation, then buries its lookup tool (the symptom index) behind roughly 1,200 words of procedure, table, figure and rules. (sts.appendix-d.b0017, sts.appendix-d.b0018, sts.appendix-d.b0022, sts.appendix-d.b0028, sts.appendix-d.b0023, sts.appendix-d.b0024) Quote (CT author voice): "The Ledger is meant to be opened mid-situation."
- **major**: It contradicts the Introduction. sts.introduction.b0152 promises each practice is 'three concrete actions, sized for a single week'. Appendix D says some take a month or a season. (sts.appendix-d.b0018) Quote (CT author voice): "Some finish in an evening; others need a month, a season, or repeated work."
- **major**: A self-help ritual laid over a history index. Read twice, say your seat out loud, teach it to someone. It reads as a seminar and invites mockery. (sts.appendix-d.b0017, sts.appendix-d.b0018) Quote (CT author voice): "You'll know the second read happened because it's slightly uncomfortable."
- **minor**: The blacklist repeats sts.chapter7.b0104 (Western Union memo, Bavarian decree) and its closing line. (sts.appendix-d.b0027) Quote (CT author voice): "The Bavarian decree deserves naming specifically: it's the most-cited example of railway technophobia on the internet, and no archival record of it has ever been produced."
- **minor**: The table and the timeline figure present the same 24 items twice in a row. (sts.appendix-d.b0022, sts.appendix-d.b0028) Quote (CT author voice (figure alt text)): "Compact index of all twenty-four precedents."

### What readers would complain about

- *skeptic*: "Twenty-four handpicked stories that all happen to agree with the author. Calling it a ledger doesn't make it a sample."
- *bored newcomer*: "A table of cute titles like 'Torches of Freedom' and 'The Mirror Twin' that mean nothing unless you've read the whole book, then twenty-four fortune cookies."
- *literary critic*: "'You'll know the second read happened because it's slightly uncomfortable.' A self-help seminar in a historian's coat."
- *hostile podcast host*: "The chart in Appendix D tells you the full titles and dates are in Appendix D. Did anyone proofread the back of this book?"
- *history buff*: "The blacklist is the only thing here I'd quote, and it's the last thing on the page."

### Weak writing

- `sts.appendix-d.b0017` "A ledger you read once is trivia. A ledger you operate is an instrument. The difference between a reader and a practitioner is procedure, not sincerity. Here's the procedure." : Stacked aphorisms, a 'not X' construction and throat-clearing before a numbered list. Four sentences to say 'use it like this'.
- `sts.appendix-d.b0018` "Say out loud which seat you're in right now. The rules exist to move you to the third." : Lecture tone, workshop-facilitator voice.
- `sts.appendix-d.b0018` "Panic spreads person to person. So does this." : A cute tag with a vague 'this'. It ends a procedure on a slogan.
- `sts.appendix-d.b0023` "P-05. A capability lasts only as long as someone sustains it, and its reach never settles its purpose." : Abstract nouns ('capability', 'reach', 'purpose'). Opaque without Chapter 3.
- `sts.appendix-d.b0023` "P-08. Spend your energy on position, not on whether, and count welfare separately from adoption." : 'Not X' plus three undefined terms ('position', 'welfare', 'adoption').
- `sts.appendix-d.b0023` "P-17. Seeing the wave isn't the hard part. Find the line item that charges people for inconvenience: it's a countdown clock." : Mixed metaphor (wave, line item, clock). It can't be parsed without Chapter 14.
- `sts.appendix-d.b0023` "P-18. Your product is mortal. Your capabilities travel, if you can name them in time and have somewhere to stand while you do." : A clause pileup. The conditional swallows the rule.
- `sts.appendix-d.b0023` "P-22. A premortem that works looks like paranoia that wasn't needed. Judge the reasoning, not the silence." : The first sentence is excellent, but the second repeats the 'not X' shape that six of the 24 rules share ('Plan by doors, not dates', 'Fight for terms, not against physics'), so the rules blur together.
- `sts.appendix-d.b0027` "History is a strong enough argument without inventing any of it." : It explains the hammer after 'Losing it costs nothing.' already landed, and it repeats the closing line of sts.chapter7.b0104. 'Survives contact with a primary source' earlier in the block is a riff on a cliché.
- `sts.appendix-d.b0024` "Your union or trade body just published a statement about AI and the art" : 'The art' is unclear. Which art? It reads like a truncated edit.

### Not in your voice

- `sts.appendix-d.b0026` "Historical interpretation stays distinct from the events it describes, company histories are labeled as company histories, and where only a catalog record or a later account was available, the entry says so." : A methods-section register. CT's draft cites nothing and talks like a friend at a bar: 'Go ahead and grab your favorite drink and your favorite tinfoil hat'.
- `sts.appendix-d.b0017` "The difference between a reader and a practitioner is procedure, not sincerity." : A polished, symmetrical epigram. CT's own 'not X' habit in the draft is breathless and enthusiastic ('This isn’t about tech support; it’s about revolutionizing curiosity.'), not clipped and aphoristic.
- `sts.appendix-d.b0018` "Name your seat." : Ritual instruction. The draft's direct address is confessional ('You're afraid. I get it.'), not a facilitator's script.
- `sts.appendix-d.b0023` "P-05. A capability lasts only as long as someone sustains it, and its reach never settles its purpose." : Abstract and bloodless. Nothing in the draft is this cold.

### Where a newcomer gets lost

- `sts.appendix-d.b0022`: The titles are teasers ('One Million Years, Give or Take', 'The Mirror Twin'). The table says nothing about what happened.
- `sts.appendix-d.b0023`: Many rules depend on their chapter's metaphor: P-12 'the cascade', P-17 'the line item', P-18 'mortal product', P-08 'position'.
- `sts.appendix-d.b0018`: 'The incumbent defending the beachfront' and 'the wave' are Chapter 14 imagery, used before they're explained.

### Skim problems

- The symptom index, the one skim-native tool, is block 11 of 13.
- Table (b0022), figure (b0028) and rules (b0023) take three passes over the same 24 IDs before anything usable appears.
- Twenty-four rule bullets of identical shape, with nothing ranked or grouped.
- 278 words of numbered procedure (b0018) on how to use an index.

### Best blocks

- `sts.appendix-d.b0024`: The most usable page in the back matter. It turns history into a lookup by situation ('Someone calls your preparation paranoid', 'A smart person just called AI a fad, a bubble, or a toy'). This is the Field Manual at its best.
- `sts.appendix-d.b0027`: Credibility armor for the skeptic. It names the fabricated quotes, including the '640K ought to be enough for anybody' line misattributed to Bill Gates, and 'Losing it costs nothing.' hits hard. sts.chapter7.b0104 depends on it.
- `sts.appendix-d.b0023`: If the precedents survive, a handful of rules are the book's best compression: 'Stop arguing about the machine's soul and start writing its invoice.' 'Don't refuse the new machine. Use it to pay for what you want to keep.'
- `sts.appendix-d.b0015`: 'Same wave, different water.' plus the honest limit: 'None of them proves a date for AGI or a law of progress.'


## Appendix E: The Cyberdeck

**Verdict.** At 1,774 words, this appendix says twice that it's optional (b0039, b0050). It duplicates Chapter 5's cyberdeck section, contradicts that section's anatomy, and pins its case to two 2026 trend pieces. The history and the four capabilities are good, and two passages are pure CT. The rest is a hedge-laden spec sheet with no prices that will be out of date before the paperback.

**Publishable as is?** No. As is, it's a dated, hedge-heavy spec sheet that repeats Chapter 5 and says nobody needs it. For the core cut it is a candidate for full removal. If sts.chapter5.b0146 ('Appendix E has more, including the easier route: the phone already in your pocket') survives, keep only b0055 and b0061, or drop that pointer block too.

### Failure modes

- **critical**: By its own admission it isn't needed, yet it costs about 3.5 percent of a 50,000-word budget and repeats sts.chapter5.b0144 (the Gibson origin) and sts.chapter5.b0119 (the anatomy). (sts.appendix-e.b0039, sts.appendix-e.b0050, sts.appendix-e.b0041, sts.appendix-e.b0057) Quote (CT author voice): "Neither one is required to take part in anything in this book."
- **major**: Its anatomy contradicts Chapter 5. In sts.chapter5.b0119 'The body' is the case (Pelican case, ammo can). Here 'The body' is an integrated handheld and the case becomes 'The shell'. Readers of both get two incompatible maps of the same machine. (sts.appendix-e.b0057, sts.appendix-e.b0051) Quote (CT author voice): "An integrated handheld such as the ClockworkPi uConsole or Hackberry Pi can supply the screen, keyboard, and battery mounting."
- **major**: A pile of hedges. Whole blocks read like a safety review written after a fact-check: every claim qualified, every part conditional on 'supported' and 'compatible'. (sts.appendix-e.b0057, sts.appendix-e.b0062, sts.appendix-e.b0067, sts.appendix-e.b0069, sts.appendix-e.b0050) Quote (CT author voice): "Cooling needs depend on that workload and the enclosure. Compute Modules need a compatible carrier board."
- **major**: Dated. The case rests on 2026 press coverage and on one kit's current product configuration. Both age within a year. (sts.appendix-e.b0043, sts.appendix-e.b0044, sts.appendix-e.b0045, sts.appendix-e.b0057) Quote (CT author voice): "In 2026, it began reaching a wider audience."
- **major**: A build guide with no prices. It tells the reader to budget and never gives a single figure per tier. (sts.appendix-e.b0057, sts.appendix-e.b0062, sts.appendix-e.b0063, sts.appendix-e.b0064) Quote (CT author voice): "Read the exact kit contents before budgeting"
- **minor**: The Tier 0 test is stated three times (paragraph, figure alt, caption), and the closing practice re-runs the Tier 1 and Tier 2 tests. (sts.appendix-e.b0061, sts.appendix-e.b0075, sts.appendix-e.b0076, sts.appendix-e.b0073) Quote (CT author voice (caption)): "Tier 0 ends with a test: can you open the reference and find what you need with the network off?"
- **minor**: 'The honest part' appears twice, which implies the rest isn't honest. The second use labels a security warning that b0066 had introduced as one of 'Two warnings'. (sts.appendix-e.b0050, sts.appendix-e.b0066, sts.appendix-e.b0067) Quote (CT author voice): "First, the honest part."
- **minor**: A sweeping cultural claim built on two trend articles and an unsourced 'a lot of them'. (sts.appendix-e.b0048, sts.appendix-e.b0052) Quote (CT author voice): "it's one of the few places in consumer culture where lots of ordinary people are voluntarily walking back out of the walled garden, on their own."
- **minor**: The origin claim invites pedants. Cyberpunk tabletop games statted out decks in detail long before 2012, so 'a hole in a novel' for thirty years is the kind of line a genre reader will contest. CT should verify it before v0.12. (sts.appendix-e.b0041) Quote (CT author voice): "He never described it in much detail, so for almost thirty years a cyberdeck was a hole in a novel"

### What readers would complain about

- *maker / hardware hobbyist*: "No parts list, no prices, no wiring diagram. 'There's no official parts list, which is the point' is what you say when you didn't write one."
- *bored newcomer*: "I wanted to build the cool computer in the picture and got a reading assignment on 18 U.S.C. 1030 and Part 15 operating conditions."
- *hostile podcast host*: "The survival book's tech appendix is pegged to a TikTok trend and a Newsweek explainer. The heading is literally 'The 2026 return.'"
- *cyberpunk fan / singularity regular*: "'For almost thirty years a cyberdeck was a hole in a novel.' Tabletop cyberpunk had decks with full stats by the end of the eighties."
- *full-book reader*: "Chapter 5 already told me what a cyberdeck is, where the word came from and what's inside it. Then the appendix told me again with the body parts renamed."

### Weak writing

- `sts.appendix-e.b0048` "That's a signal. Signals like that are why you watch culture and not just earnings calls." : It explains the joke. The Create Over Consume line already landed, and this repeats it as a moral.
- `sts.appendix-e.b0052` "The interesting part isn't the look. It's the stated reason." : A textbook 'not X, it's Y' construction used as throat-clearing.
- `sts.appendix-e.b0052` "Ask the people building these why, and a lot of them give you the argument this book has been making" : An unsourced, self-flattering overclaim.
- `sts.appendix-e.b0050` "A well-documented deck can give you more control over those choices, though it still needs supported software and obtainable parts." : A hedge stacked on a hedge. The sentence gives control and takes it back.
- `sts.appendix-e.b0051` "There's no official parts list, which is the point. But most decks have the same five organs." : The organ metaphor collapses at once: 'The loadout' and 'The shell' aren't organs. 'Which is the point' dodges writing a list.
- `sts.appendix-e.b0062` "Terrain, placement, power, and settings decide whether the link works." : A noun list used as a caveat, with nothing a reader can act on.
- `sts.appendix-e.b0070` "Build the deck to keep yourself free, not to reach into anyone else's life. The tool doesn't decide that for you. You do." : 'Not X' followed by a sermon tag. Lecture tone.
- `sts.appendix-e.b0072` "not a computer you're allowed to use, but a computer you understand, can repair, and can't be locked out of." : A textbook 'not X but Y' plus a list of three, and the same triplet appears in Appendix D (P-21) and Appendix G (b0018). It's a slogan on loop.
- `sts.appendix-e.b0044` "In 2026, it began reaching a wider audience." : A limp, standalone press-release sentence.

### Not in your voice

- `sts.appendix-e.b0057` "Match the power supply to the exact board and peripherals, then check temperature and runtime under your workload." : Spec-sheet compliance prose. The draft never qualifies anything ('a LOT closer than you think') and sells excitement ('the single most exhilarating time to be alive').
- `sts.appendix-e.b0062` "Default channel keys are public; use a randomly generated shared key for a private group and decide whether location sharing belongs on." : A configuration manual. Nothing in CT's draft reads like documentation.
- `sts.appendix-e.b0069` "Some transmissions need an individual license; others are allowed only with compliant equipment and operating conditions." : Statute voice. The draft's register is a friend talking, with profanity and jokes.
- `sts.appendix-e.b0067` "Encryption protects stored data while it is locked; it doesn't make an unlocked or compromised computer safe." : Uncontracted 'it is' and security-engineer cadence. The VOICE-GUIDE flags uncontracted prose as drift.

### Where a newcomer gets lost

- `sts.appendix-e.b0055`: 'A pocket version of the Crucible from Chapter 11', 'the co-op's network' and 'the Chapter 17 storm' are story callbacks a cold reader can't decode.
- `sts.appendix-e.b0055`: 'Software-defined radio', 'LoRa' and 'Meshtastic' are used without definition here. They're only in the Glossary.
- `sts.appendix-e.b0062`: 'Flashed with the firmware' and 'Default channel keys' assume maker knowledge.
- `sts.appendix-e.b0057`: 'Compute Modules need a compatible carrier board' means nothing to a newcomer.
- `sts.appendix-e.b0048`: 'The smart-home trap from Chapter 17' and 'Create Over Consume' are book jargon, unexplained here.

### Skim problems

- The Tier 0 test appears three times (b0061, b0075, b0076), and the Tier 1 and 2 tests repeat in the practice (b0073).
- The tier paragraphs are dense 50 to 85 word blocks with bold leads. The actual step is buried mid-paragraph.
- Inline citation links break the reading line in b0045, b0057, b0060, b0062, b0067 and b0069.
- The thing a skimmer looks for, what to buy and what it costs, doesn't exist.

### Best blocks

- `sts.appendix-e.b0041`: 'A hole in a novel, shaped exactly like the machine you wished you owned' is the best sentence in the back matter. It's a pure CT image.
- `sts.appendix-e.b0048`: The first two sentences are CT at full voice: 'It's Create Over Consume wearing a case somebody printed at two in the morning.'
- `sts.appendix-e.b0055`: The four capabilities (local knowledge, intelligence, communication, spectrum) are the real survival case. 'The librarian is still in your pack' is a keeper.
- `sts.appendix-e.b0061`: Tier 0 is free, doable tonight, and uses the phone you already own. It's the most newcomer-friendly action in the appendix.
- `sts.appendix-e.b0058`: The lithium warning is short, specific and correct.
- `sts.appendix-e.b0042`: A compact, dated and concrete history: a thirty-five-dollar board in 2012.


## Appendix F: Glossary

**Verdict.** A serviceable glossary. Its social and economic entries are crisp. Its technical entries read as if a fact-checker edited them into caveats that define by negation. It claims 'Every term this book leans on' and misses LLM, token, UBI, the ladder and its rungs, and the co-op cast, while finding room for Photobioreactor. Every entry ends in a chapter pointer, so it breaks wherever the core cut removes material.

**Publishable as is?** Only with fixes. The social entries are publishable now. The technical entries (Transformer, Open weights, Model collapse, DC-native, Mesh network) need CT's own rewrite, which a block cut can't supply, and b0002's coverage claim is false. In the core cut, every entry whose chapter is cut has to go with it.

### Failure modes

- **major**: False coverage claim. Missing: LLM / large language model (used in the book), token (used inside the Transformer entry itself), UBI (the obvious comparison the UBS entry dodges), the ladder and rungs (used in Appendices C, G and H), Tier 0 to 3, and Elijah, Marta or 'the co-op' (the Crucible entry refers to 'the story' and never says whose). (sts.appendix-f.b0002, sts.appendix-f.b0046, sts.appendix-f.b0047, sts.appendix-f.b0011) Quote (CT author voice): "Every term this book leans on, in plain words, with the chapter that explains it."
- **major**: Hedge-edited entries define by caveat or negation instead of meaning. (sts.appendix-f.b0046, sts.appendix-f.b0033, sts.appendix-f.b0028, sts.appendix-f.b0014, sts.appendix-f.b0009, sts.appendix-f.b0027, sts.appendix-f.b0015) Quote (CT author voice): "It isn't an inevitable result of every use of synthetic data."
- **major**: The Transformer entry fails the newcomer completely. It is the one AI concept a first-timer would look up, and it explains jargon with more jargon. (sts.appendix-f.b0046) Quote (CT author voice): "Autoregressive language models still generate their response a token at a time."
- **major**: Chapter pointers will dangle in the core cut. Entries sending readers to a chapter story that may be gone become broken promises. (sts.appendix-f.b0034, sts.appendix-f.b0011, sts.appendix-f.b0030, sts.appendix-f.b0006) Quote (CT author voice): "The builder in Chapter 17 printed one at home."
- **minor**: It duplicates Appendix C almost verbatim (definition, schools, Adequate Level of Care, food first, nine stages). (sts.appendix-f.b0042, sts.appendix-f.b0004, sts.appendix-f.b0019, sts.appendix-f.b0003, sts.appendix-f.b0017, sts.appendix-f.b0022, sts.appendix-f.b0032) Quote (CT author voice): "A place to start, not a waiting list."
- **minor**: The Primate Backlash entry drops Chapter 2's 'the primates are all of us' and reads as contempt for people angry about losing work. (sts.appendix-f.b0037) Quote (CT author voice): "Stage 5: grief and anger at the change, including real objections, sometimes expressed with a brick."
- **minor**: Third-person 'the author', again. (sts.appendix-f.b0005) Quote (CT author voice (third person)): "The author's forecast for it is U.S. Thanksgiving, November 25, 2027; it's a forecast, not a finding."
- **minor**: SB 1383's 20 percent target is given with no target year, in a book set in 2026. A skeptic will ask whether it was met. (sts.appendix-f.b0039) Quote (CT author voice): "setting a target to recover 20 percent of edible food that would otherwise be landfilled."

### What readers would complain about

- *bored newcomer*: "I looked up 'Transformer' to finally understand ChatGPT and came out needing a second glossary for 'autoregressive' and 'token.'"
- *singularity regular*: "A Singularity glossary with 'Photobioreactor' and 'Shouse' and no LLM, no compute, no scaling."
- *skeptic*: "Half these definitions are disclaimers. Don't tell me what model collapse isn't. Tell me what it is."
- *displaced worker*: "'Primate Backlash: grief and anger at the change.' So my grief gets a monkey label."
- *short-route reader*: "The glossary keeps sending me to chapters I was told I could skip."

### Weak writing

- `sts.appendix-f.b0046` "Attention relates positions in a sequence and permits more parallel computation during training than earlier recurrent designs." : Academic drift. It defines a term with three undefined ones (attention, parallel computation, recurrent).
- `sts.appendix-f.b0033` "Downloaded copies can run locally on suitable hardware, but availability alone doesn't grant unrestricted rights or provide the training data." : A lawyer's hedge inside a definition.
- `sts.appendix-f.b0014` "Charge controllers, voltage converters, batteries, and wiring still have losses." : A caveat stapled to a definition. The reader learns what the thing doesn't do.
- `sts.appendix-f.b0009` "Other uses need their own agreements." : An empty sentence with no content.
- `sts.appendix-f.b0027` "An alternate working path can carry traffic around a failure; without one, part of the network can still go dark." : Clunky ('alternate working path'), and the hedge is the longest part of the entry.
- `sts.appendix-f.b0015` "Not isolation, and not the same as "decentralization."" : Definition by negation. Two 'not's where a picture should be.
- `sts.appendix-f.b0044` "The hinge of the nine stages, where a society decides how the gains from automation reach the people affected." : Stacked abstractions ('a society', 'the gains', 'the people affected').

### Not in your voice

- `sts.appendix-f.b0046` "Autoregressive language models still generate their response a token at a time." : CT explains AI by picture ('Imagine a world where Einstein is your entry-level tech support.'). This is a paper abstract.
- `sts.appendix-f.b0033` "availability alone doesn't grant unrestricted rights or provide the training data." : Licensing-counsel voice. The draft has no hedges at all.
- `sts.appendix-f.b0005` "The author's forecast for it is U.S. Thanksgiving, November 25, 2027" : CT says I and mine, in the draft and even in Appendix G ('Add mine.'). Third person is a press-release tell.
- `sts.appendix-f.b0028` "It isn't an inevitable result of every use of synthetic data." : Defensive negation written for a reviewer, not a reader. The draft's tone is never defensive.

### Where a newcomer gets lost

- `sts.appendix-f.b0046`: 'Token', 'autoregressive' and 'recurrent' are undefined.
- `sts.appendix-f.b0011`: 'The co-op's local AI server in the story': which story, which co-op? There's no entry for Elijah or the co-op.
- `sts.appendix-f.b0032`: Only Stage 1's name is given. Stages 2 and 3 have no entries, though Appendix C and H lean on them.
- `sts.appendix-f.b0047`: It says UBS 'differs from a cash payment' but never names UBI, the comparison every newcomer will reach for.
- `sts.appendix-f.b0006`: 'Recommendation system' is undefined.

### Skim problems

- Depth is uneven: Shouse gets 13 words, Transformer 45. The caveat-heavy technical entries are the longest, so the eye lands on the least useful text.
- Every entry ends in a chapter parenthetical, which is noise for a reader who just wants the meaning.
- There are no see-also links between overlapping entries. Hyper-local, Deglobalization, Short loop and Neighborhood factory say nearly the same thing four ways.

### Best blocks

- `sts.appendix-f.b0042`: The book's definition of the Singularity, stated cleanly.
- `sts.appendix-f.b0016`: 'Needing the hierarchy to hold, or needing the suffering to stop' is a sharp, humane distinction in one line.
- `sts.appendix-f.b0026`: 'They read what the machines would do to their pay correctly and chose a strategy that failed.' Punchy, fair and memorable.
- `sts.appendix-f.b0029`: 'Not abolished. Optional.' carries the book's signature line into the back matter.
- `sts.appendix-f.b0041`: Shouse in 13 words: the model for every other entry.
- `sts.appendix-f.b0031`: 'Extra electricity isn't automatically dinner' is a concrete correction a newcomer will remember.
- `sts.appendix-f.b0004`: The yardstick with the 'bad week' test that makes it real.


## Appendix G: Your First Year

**Verdict.** The right idea, a year of small actions, written in a private language: tin, ratchet, beachfront audit, Fujifilm question, tavern test, filing office, household Y2K, frontier hour, Rung 1 page. Without the precedents it's a list of passwords. With them it's 46 actions sold as 'a handful', several of which assume you own your home.

**Publishable as is?** Only with fixes. If the core cut keeps the precedent practices in the chapters, G is redundant. If the cut drops them, G's callbacks become undecodable. Either way the cut has to choose one home for the practices. Block-level, keep b0004, b0010, b0016, b0024 and b0026, and drop the intro, figure and caption trio (b0002, b0028, b0029), which say the same thing three times.

### Failure modes

- **critical**: Callback jargon makes it unusable without the precedent chapters. In a 50,000-word cut that trims precedents, most action verbs point at nothing. (sts.appendix-g.b0006, sts.appendix-g.b0008, sts.appendix-g.b0010, sts.appendix-g.b0012, sts.appendix-g.b0016, sts.appendix-g.b0020, sts.appendix-g.b0022, sts.appendix-g.b0026) Quote (CT author voice): "Map your tin: the five things your household can't go a month without, and where each one actually comes from."
- **major**: It promises 'a handful' and delivers 46 actions across twelve months. Month 1 alone has five, including two separate files for AI predictions. (sts.appendix-g.b0002, sts.appendix-g.b0004, sts.appendix-g.b0008) Quote (CT author voice): "twelve months, each with a theme and a handful of actions."
- **major**: It assumes an owner, not a renter: insulation, a panel and battery, a plot, a buy-or-lease decision. (sts.appendix-g.b0012, sts.appendix-g.b0018, sts.appendix-g.b0022) Quote (CT author voice): "Move one expense from rented energy to owned energy: insulation, a bicycle, a properly sized panel and battery."
- **minor**: The discomfort-as-compass trope, the fourth time in the back matter (also Appendix D b0018 and Appendix H b0002). This item nearly repeats the Conclusion's own P-24 practice. (sts.appendix-g.b0026) Quote (CT author voice): "That discomfort is a compass. Do that one first."
- **minor**: Compliance hedges inside actions blunt them. (sts.appendix-g.b0014, sts.appendix-g.b0016, sts.appendix-g.b0018) Quote (CT author voice): "Record agreed contact details and limits, and ask before sharing them."
- **minor**: Intro, figure and caption say the same thing three times. (sts.appendix-g.b0002, sts.appendix-g.b0028, sts.appendix-g.b0029) Quote (CT author voice (caption)): "Shift the months to fit your season and your life"
- **minor**: How to Use routes parents here, and nothing in the twelve months is about children. (sts.appendix-g.b0002) Quote (CT author voice): "Chapters 8, 14, and 19, then Appendix G"

### What readers would complain about

- *bored newcomer*: "'Map your tin.' 'Run the beachfront audit.' 'Ask the Fujifilm question.' Month two of a plan written in a language I don't speak."
- *renter*: "Month 8 wants me to put in insulation and a panel and battery. I rent. Month 10: buy, lease, join, or stay. Thanks."
- *parent sent here by How to Use*: "The book told me to come here because I'm raising kids. Twelve months, forty-six actions, zero about kids."
- *literary critic*: "Promised 'a handful of actions.' I counted forty-six."

### Weak writing

- `sts.appendix-g.b0002` "It isn't a complete checklist of all seventy-two." : It defines itself by what it isn't. Padding.
- `sts.appendix-g.b0010` "Ask someone at least fifteen years younger than you who uses AI daily for twenty minutes of watching them work." : It misparses as 'uses AI daily for twenty minutes'. The request is buried at the end.
- `sts.appendix-g.b0006` "the share of income and hours you'll put into skills, soil, savings, and community." : An alliterative list for rhythm, not meaning.
- `sts.appendix-g.b0006` "Run the energy audit on paper: what comes in (calories, kilowatt-hours, dollars), what goes out (labor, attention, rent), and who captures the spread." : Nested lists of three plus finance jargon ('captures the spread').
- `sts.appendix-g.b0008` "assume that protection is temporary and plan accordingly." : 'Plan accordingly' is exactly the non-instruction the Field Manual rule forbids.
- `sts.appendix-g.b0008` "Knowledge is leverage while it's still yours to trade." : A slogan tag after an instruction.
- `sts.appendix-g.b0022` "the day you'll have made a position decision, buy, lease, join, or stay." : Jargon ('position decision') and a comma list where a colon belongs.
- `sts.appendix-g.b0026` "That discomfort is a compass." : A cliché, repeated across appendices.

### Not in your voice

- `sts.appendix-g.b0014` "Ask willing neighbors what help they can offer: repair, growing, transport, qualified medical care, or licensed electrical work." : HR-policy diction ('qualified', 'licensed'). The draft's register is 'Go ahead and grab your favorite drink and your favorite tinfoil hat'.
- `sts.appendix-g.b0016` "Publish totals with units, periods, sources, and gaps. Keep personal records private." : A data-governance memo. CT doesn't write like this when he writes alone.
- `sts.appendix-g.b0002` "This year plan selects and adapts some of them, alongside a few chapter exercises" : Housekeeping prose about the document. The draft never describes itself. It just goes ('Let's break it down.').

### Where a newcomer gets lost

- `sts.appendix-g.b0006`: 'Map your tin' (Chapter 9) and 'household Y2K' (Chapter 18) are undefined here.
- `sts.appendix-g.b0008`: 'Capability inventory' (P-18) and 'beachfront audit' (P-17) are unexplained.
- `sts.appendix-g.b0010`: 'Frontier hour' (Chapter 3) and 'the Communicator' (Appendix E) are unexplained.
- `sts.appendix-g.b0012`: 'Off the ratchet' (Chapter 6) means nothing on its own.
- `sts.appendix-g.b0016`: 'The Rung 1 page from Chapter 19' assumes the ladder.
- `sts.appendix-g.b0020`: 'Tavern test' and 'own your printing press' (Chapter 16) are callbacks.
- `sts.appendix-g.b0022`: 'Open the filing office' (Chapter 12) and 'position decision' (Chapter 6) are callbacks.
- `sts.appendix-g.b0026`: 'The Fujifilm question' (Chapter 14) and 'the standby system' (Chapter 15) are callbacks.

### Skim problems

- The month headings are excellent skim anchors. The actions under them aren't, because the lead verbs are callbacks ('Run the beachfront audit', 'Open the filing office').
- A P-tag after almost every action is noise to anyone without the precedents.
- The figure (b0028) is a picture of the twelve headings that follow it.

### Best blocks

- `sts.appendix-g.b0004`: Doable today, no jargon in item 1 ('put the phone in a drawer that closes, and do one real thing before noon'), and 'Add mine.' is CT owning his forecast in first person.
- `sts.appendix-g.b0024`: The book's political ask made concrete and brave: 'say out loud, on the record, that the people who live here should get what they need to survive.'
- `sts.appendix-g.b0010`: 'Say nothing for the first ten.' and 'A document, a repaired fence, a loaf.' are concrete and humane.
- `sts.appendix-g.b0016`: Count the food is the core thesis turned into an action, and item 3 (ask your grocer what happens to unsold food) needs no backstory.
- `sts.appendix-g.b0026`: Date-stamping your reasoning is a strong, original habit, and it closes the year with an honest look back.


## Appendix H: Talk About It

**Verdict.** Standard back matter that does no harm and no essential work. One question is pinned to a June 2026 news cycle in a garbled sentence. The reading list hands every hostile reader the 'this is socialism' clip while giving the singularity reader two AI items out of twelve. It's the first appendix to go in a 50,000-word cut.

**Publishable as is?** Yes, as conventional back matter. Nothing in it is wrong. The dated Fable 5 / Mythos 5 question sits inside b0006 with three good questions, so a block cut can't remove it alone, and CT has to handle it in v0.12. For the 50,000-word core it is the most expendable 990 words in the back matter. No other block in the book depends on it except sts.how-to-use.b0007's mention.

### Failure modes

- **major**: A dated, garbled question embedded in an otherwise good block. It will be unintelligible within a year, and its last clause is opaque without Chapter 2. (sts.appendix-h.b0006) Quote (CT author voice): "Chapter 2 recounts the June 2026 export controls on Fable 5 and Mythos 5 and their lifting eighteen days later, while access to the two models still differed."
- **major**: Reading-list optics and thin AI coverage. Wilde's socialism essay, Kropotkin, Bevan, and Keynes linked through marxists.org give the 'Isn't this just socialism?' objector (answered in appendix-c b0049) a citation trail. Only two of twelve items (Vinge, Grace) are about AI, so the singularity reader gets nothing to catch up on. (sts.appendix-h.b0015) Quote (CT author voice (naming Oscar Wilde's essay)): "The Soul of Man under Socialism (1891)."
- **minor**: 'The nine stages' and 'the nine rungs' both appear as discussion prompts with no reminder of which is which. (sts.appendix-h.b0006, sts.appendix-h.b0012) Quote (CT author voice): "Which of the nine rungs is your town already standing on, even partly?"
- **minor**: Third-person 'The author says', again. (sts.appendix-h.b0004) Quote (CT author voice (third person, quoting his own Introduction)): "The author says "we don't have to live like this.""
- **minor**: A small overclaim on the Grace survey. The paper surveyed published AI authors, as its own title says, not specifically the people building these systems. (sts.appendix-h.b0015) Quote (CT author voice): "What the people building these systems actually expect, and how fast their forecasts moved."
- **minor**: The discomfort trope opens the appendix, the third or fourth time in the back matter. (sts.appendix-h.b0002) Quote (CT author voice): "The best conversations usually start with the one that makes somebody in the room uncomfortable."

### What readers would complain about

- *hostile podcast host*: "The reading list links Keynes through marxists.org and recommends Kropotkin and Wilde's 'The Soul of Man under Socialism.' Then Appendix C swears it isn't socialism."
- *singularity regular*: "Twelve books, two about AI. No Kurzweil, no Bostrom, no Yudkowsky, even though Appendix C name-checks them."
- *book club host*: "Half the questions need the chapter open, and the export-control one will mean nothing to my group by next year."

### Weak writing

- `sts.appendix-h.b0006` "and their lifting eighteen days later, while access to the two models still differed." : Tangled syntax. 'While access... still differed' means nothing without Chapter 2.
- `sts.appendix-h.b0010` "France's 2016 law prohibited deliberately spoiling edible surplus and required qualifying retailers to offer donation agreements." : A legal paraphrase ('qualifying retailers', 'donation agreements'). A discussion prompt shouldn't read like a statute summary.
- `sts.appendix-h.b0012` "The Conclusion asks: how will the people affected share in the benefit?" : Stacked abstractions. It's the least discussable question in the list.
- `sts.appendix-h.b0002` "The best conversations usually start with the one that makes somebody in the room uncomfortable." : A repeated trope stated as a truism.

### Not in your voice

- `sts.appendix-h.b0004` "The author says "we don't have to live like this."" : Third person. The draft is all I and you ('I vote for the latter').
- `sts.appendix-h.b0006` "What evidence would distinguish an overreaction from a justified precaution?" : Seminar-room diction. The draft asks its questions bluntly ('What the fuck is going on?', 'Are we ready to share the planet with something we created but can’t fully understand or control?').
- `sts.appendix-h.b0010` "required qualifying retailers to offer donation agreements." : A legal brief, not CT.

### Where a newcomer gets lost

- `sts.appendix-h.b0006`: The question never says what Fable 5 and Mythos 5 are.
- `sts.appendix-h.b0006`: 'A door gives you a Monday' is quoted with no gloss.
- `sts.appendix-h.b0012`: 'The nine rungs' assumes Chapter 19's ladder.
- `sts.appendix-h.b0008`: 'The Entitled' and 'the Ready' are book terms without a gloss here.

### Skim problems

- The reading list has no grouping (economics, AI, history), so a reader hunting for the AI reading finds it at items seven and nine of twelve.
- The question headings repeat the Part titles, which is fine, but no question is marked as the essential one, so a group with twenty minutes doesn't know where to start.

### Best blocks

- `sts.appendix-h.b0008`: Concrete and personal: 'Is your trade running its own version now? Would you sign the coupon?' This is the best discussion prompt in the book.
- `sts.appendix-h.b0012`: The co-op and NHS questions turn the plan into local, answerable questions ('What need in your community doesn't get met because meeting it doesn't pay?').
- `sts.appendix-h.b0015`: The annotations are tight one-liners, most of the list is free, and Wilde and Keynes are squarely on thesis. It includes the Mackay line about people recovering their senses 'slowly, and one by one' (Charles Mackay, real person, quoted).
- `sts.appendix-h.b0004`: 'What would change in your own week if you didn't need money to eat? What wouldn't?' makes the core idea personal.
